Hi. Yeah, thanks. Um, well, I guess I'm here because work has just been... a lot lately. I work at the information desk in this big commercial building downtown. I've been there for a few years. I actually took some classes at the community college before that, thinking I'd do IT, but the benefits at this desk job are pretty decent, you know? Anyway, it used to be mostly quiet, just some accounting firms, but they recently leased the second floor to this massive tech company. And man, the amount of food delivery guys coming in and out now is insane. They never read the signs about where to leave the bags, so I'm always having to point them to the right table. 

But, um, sorry, what really pushed me to make the appointment is that... well, every time one of those heavy lobby doors slams, or if someone rolls a suitcase fast across the marble floor—we get a lot of visiting consultants with those hard-shell bags now—I just jump. I jump out of my skin like it’s happening again. And it takes me a good minute just to get my heart back down to normal. It's just exhausting.

Um, well, thinking about going in... I guess a little? Mostly I just try to change how I get to my desk. Like, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can, because walking past that specific spot in the main lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it as fast as possible. So the back hallway is just easier. It routes you right past the loading dock, actually, which is where Gary, our maintenance guy, usually takes his breaks. Gary's this older guy, always wearing these heavy-duty suspenders, which you really don't see a lot of guys wearing anymore. He told me he has to order them special from some hardware catalog because the regular store-bought ones don't hold up when he's carrying his tool belt. He's a funny guy, always has a story about some pipe that burst back in the 90s. Oh, and that back hallway has this old vending machine, too—the kind with the metal coils that always get stuck? I actually lost a dollar fifty in there trying to get some Cool Ranch Doritos last Tuesday. It just totally ate my money and left the bag dangling there. I tried shaking it, but that thing weighs a ton. But anyway, yeah, I just take that route now so I don't have to go through the lobby.

Um, intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think I've had anything like that. Just the startle thing at work, really. But speaking of things happening fast, my sister's dog did that the other day. She has this golden retriever mix, Buster, and he is usually the laziest dog you'll ever meet. I mean, he literally sleeps on his back with his paws in the air like a dead bug. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Which is funny because she actually buys him this really expensive organic dog food that's supposed to be made with real salmon and sweet potatoes. It smells awful, honestly. I don't know how he eats it. I told her she should just give him regular kibble, but she's really into the whole holistic pet care thing now. She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.

Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said "My other ride is the Millennium Falcon," which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.

I don't think so, anyway. Most of the time I'm pretty relaxed at the desk, just doing my thing. I actually started doing the New York Times crossword to pass the time when it's slow. My buddy Mike got me into it. He works at this coffee shop around the corner—it's one of those independent places that charges like seven bucks for a latte, you know? They use oat milk by default now, which I just don't get. I tried it once and it honestly just tasted like leftover cereal water. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. 

She wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. 

But anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.

They never read the signs about where to leave the bags, so I'm always having to point them to the right table. \n\nBut, um, sorry, what really pushed me to make the appointment is that... well, every time one of those heavy lobby doors slams, or if someone rolls a suitcase fast across the marble floor—we get a lot of visiting consultants with those hard-shell bags now—I just jump. I jump out of my skin like it’s happening again. And it takes me a good minute just to get my heart back down to normal. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: When those loud noises startle you, do you ever feel like you're back in a different time or place?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm somewhere else or anything. I just... I get this massive jolt, you know? Like my body thinks something terrible is happening right that second. But I know I'm still at the desk. The desk is actually made of this really nice polished granite, which sounds fancy, but it's freezing in the winter. The building management keeps the AC blasting all year round anyway, so I'm always wearing this thick cardigan my sister got me for Christmas a couple of years ago. She lives up in Seattle now, so she's all about layers. I keep telling her she needs to come visit, but she hates flying. She had this terrible delay once in O'Hare where she was stuck for like twelve hours... but yeah, no, I don't feel like I'm back in time or anything, just really, really jumpy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at other times, not just when you're startled by loud noises?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. Most of the time I'm pretty relaxed at the desk, just doing my thing. I actually started doing the New York Times crossword to pass the time when it's slow. My buddy Mike got me into it. He works at this coffee shop around the corner—it's one of those independent places that charges like seven bucks for a latte, you know? They use oat milk by default now, which I just don't get. I tried it once and it honestly just tasted like leftover cereal water. I really don't know how people drink that every single morning, especially for that price. But anyway, Mike usually saves me the leftover papers from the café so I can do the puzzles. So yeah, usually I'm just sitting there working on a crossword, totally fine. It's really just when those sudden noises happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really worry about much outside of work. I go home, I watch a little TV, maybe play some video games. I actually just picked up this old console at a garage sale last weekend. It was one of those community yard sales where everyone in the neighborhood puts their stuff out on the same day. I love those. You can find some crazy stuff. This guy had a whole box of old vinyl records, mostly 80s pop, which isn't really my thing. I'm more into classic rock. My dad used to play Zeppelin all the time when he was working on his car in the garage. He had this old Mustang he was always fixing up. Never quite got it running right, though. It was always leaking oil or something. But yeah, outside of work, I'm pretty chill. I don't really sit around worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when anticipating or thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, thinking about going in... I guess a little? Mostly I just try to change how I get to my desk. Like, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can, because walking past that specific spot in the main lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it as fast as possible. So the back hallway is just easier. It routes you right past the loading dock, actually, which is where Gary, our maintenance guy, usually takes his breaks. Gary's this older guy, always wearing these heavy-duty suspenders, which you really don't see a lot of guys wearing anymore. He told me he has to order them special from some hardware catalog because the regular store-bought ones don't hold up when he's carrying his tool belt. He's a funny guy, always has a story about some pipe that burst back in the 90s. Oh, and that back hallway has this old vending machine, too—the kind with the metal coils that always get stuck? I actually lost a dollar fifty in there trying to get some Cool Ranch Doritos last Tuesday. It just totally ate my money and left the bag dangling there. I tried shaking it, but that thing weighs a ton. But anyway, yeah, I just take that route now so I don't have to go through the lobby.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think I've had anything like that. Just the startle thing at work, really. But speaking of things happening fast, my sister's dog did that the other day. She has this golden retriever mix, Buster, and he is usually the laziest dog you'll ever meet. I mean, he literally sleeps on his back with his paws in the air like a dead bug. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Which is funny because she actually buys him this really expensive organic dog food that's supposed to be made with real salmon and sweet potatoes. It smells awful, honestly. I don't know how he eats it. I told her she should just give him regular kibble, but she's really into the whole holistic pet care thing now. She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about different things in your life, not just related to work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I think we just talked about that, actually. Like I said, outside of work, I'm pretty relaxed. I don't really worry about much. Oh, except I am a little worried about my car right now. It's been making this weird rattling noise when I get up over like, 50 miles an hour. It sounds like it's coming from the front passenger side, maybe near the tire? I don't know. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment—Mike's Auto, I think it's called. Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about several different things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, like money or relationships?\n\nPatient: Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. \n\nShe wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific at work that you worry about or fear happening?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can lately. Walking past that specific spot in the lobby where it happened... my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it. Which is a huge pain because the back hallway smells exactly like old mop water. You know that smell? Like someone left a wet sponge in a bucket for a month. The maintenance guy, Gary, keeps all his supplies back there. \n\nGary's a good guy, though. He actually brought in donuts on Friday. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. I had to use like three squirts of hand sanitizer just to answer the phones at the front desk without getting my keyboard all gross. The keyboard is already kind of gross anyway because the temp who covers my lunch breaks eats Cheetos while she types. The flaming hot ones, too. I keep finding red dust on the spacebar and it drives me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I really try to avoid that side of the lobby now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Okay, seriously, I feel like I'm on a hidden camera show or something. Are you just reading the same question off a different list? Because no, I really don't worry about my health or my bank account or relationships when I'm at home. I'm totally fine.

You'd think a regular doctor would tell you that, not a dentist. Dr. Henderson, he's a trip. I mean, I don't really worry about much outside of work. I go home, I watch a little TV, maybe play some video games. I actually just picked up this old console at a garage sale last weekend. It was one of those community yard sales where everyone in the neighborhood puts their stuff out on the same day. I love those. You can find some crazy stuff. This guy had a whole box of old vinyl records, mostly 80s pop, which isn't really my thing. I'm more into classic rock. My dad used to play Zeppelin all the time when he was working on his car in the garage. He had this old Mustang he was always fixing up. Never quite got it running right, though. It was always leaking oil or something. But yeah, outside of work, I'm pretty chill. I don't really sit around worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when anticipating or thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, thinking about going in... I guess a little? Mostly I just try to change how I get to my desk. Like, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can, because walking past that specific spot in the main lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it as fast as possible. So the back hallway is just easier. It routes you right past the loading dock, actually, which is where Gary, our maintenance guy, usually takes his breaks. Gary's this older guy, always wearing these heavy-duty suspenders, which you really don't see a lot of guys wearing anymore. He told me he has to order them special from some hardware catalog because the regular store-bought ones don't hold up when he's carrying his tool belt. He's a funny guy, always has a story about some pipe that burst back in the 90s. Oh, and that back hallway has this old vending machine, too—the kind with the metal coils that always get stuck? I actually lost a dollar fifty in there trying to get some Cool Ranch Doritos last Tuesday. It just totally ate my money and left the bag dangling there. I tried shaking it, but that thing weighs a ton. But anyway, yeah, I just take that route now so I don't have to go through the lobby.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think I've had anything like that. Just the startle thing at work, really. But speaking of things happening fast, my sister's dog did that the other day. She has this golden retriever mix, Buster, and he is usually the laziest dog you'll ever meet. I mean, he literally sleeps on his back with his paws in the air like a dead bug. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Which is funny because she actually buys him this really expensive organic dog food that's supposed to be made with real salmon and sweet potatoes. It smells awful, honestly. I don't know how he eats it. I told her she should just give him regular kibble, but she's really into the whole holistic pet care thing now. She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about different things in your life, not just related to work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I think we just talked about that, actually. Like I said, outside of work, I'm pretty relaxed. I don't really worry about much. Oh, except I am a little worried about my car right now. It's been making this weird rattling noise when I get up over like, 50 miles an hour. It sounds like it's coming from the front passenger side, maybe near the tire? I don't know. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment—Mike's Auto, I think it's called. Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about several different things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, like money or relationships?\n\nPatient: Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. \n\nShe wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. I had to use like three squirts of hand sanitizer just to answer the phones at the front desk without getting my keyboard all gross. The keyboard is already kind of gross anyway because the temp who covers my lunch breaks eats Cheetos while she types. The flaming hot ones, too. I keep finding red dust on the spacebar and it drives me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I'm totally fine. \n\nI mean, speaking of health, I did switch to 2% milk recently instead of whole milk. My dentist actually told me to drink more milk. Which is weird, right? You'd think a regular doctor would tell you that, not a dentist. Dr. Henderson, he's a trip. He has these little TVs mounted on the ceiling above the dentist chair so you can watch Netflix while he scrapes your teeth. Last time I was there, he had on that baking show, the British one with the big white tent in the middle of a field. It's honestly so relaxing. Though it always makes me crave pastries, which kind of defeats the entire purpose of being at the dentist. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, are you okay? Like, genuinely, are we just doing laps around the same track here? I swear I've answered this exact thing like four times. No, I don't have trouble controlling my worry about everyday stuff, because I'm not worrying about everyday stuff. 

It's actually kind of funny, my buddy Dave asks me the same questions over and over sometimes. But that's because Dave has the memory of a goldfish. We were supposed to go see that new sci-fi movie on Saturday—the one with the aliens that communicate through, like, radio static or whatever? But he completely forgot and double-booked himself for his niece's piano recital. Which, honestly, sounds like a form of torture. Sitting in a stuffy auditorium listening to a bunch of eight-year-olds butcher 'Chopsticks' for two hours? I'd rather watch paint dry. Dave said they didn't even have snacks afterward, just warm tap water in those tiny paper cones. You know the ones? The cones that you literally can't set down anywhere without spilling water all over yourself. Who even invented those? It's a terrible design. 

But yeah, anyway, to answer your question for the fifth time, no. I'm not sitting around worrying about normal stuff. I'm totally fine with all that.

She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about different things in your life, not just related to work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I think we just talked about that, actually. Like I said, outside of work, I'm pretty relaxed. I don't really worry about much. Oh, except I am a little worried about my car right now. It's been making this weird rattling noise when I get up over like, 50 miles an hour. It sounds like it's coming from the front passenger side, maybe near the tire? I don't know. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment—Mike's Auto, I think it's called. Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about several different things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, like money or relationships?\n\nPatient: Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. \n\nShe wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific at work that you worry about or fear happening?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can lately. Walking past that specific spot in the lobby where it happened... my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it. Which is a huge pain because the back hallway smells exactly like old mop water. You know that smell? Like someone left a wet sponge in a bucket for a month. The maintenance guy, Gary, keeps all his supplies back there. \n\nGary's a good guy, though. He actually brought in donuts on Friday. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. I had to use like three squirts of hand sanitizer just to answer the phones at the front desk without getting my keyboard all gross. I keep finding red dust on the spacebar and it drives me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I'm totally fine. \n\nI mean, speaking of health, I did switch to 2% milk recently instead of whole milk. My dentist actually told me to drink more milk. Which is weird, right? You'd think a regular doctor would tell you that, not a dentist. Dr. Henderson, he's a trip. No, not at all. It's really just the stuff at work. Outside of that, I'm totally fine. Like, even if there are loud noises elsewhere, it doesn't do that to me.

I jump out of my skin like it’s happening again. And it takes me a good minute just to get my heart back down to normal. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: When those loud noises startle you, do you ever feel like you're back in a different time or place?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm somewhere else or anything. I just... I get this massive jolt, you know? Like my body thinks something terrible is happening right that second. But I know I'm still at the desk. The desk is actually made of this really nice polished granite, which sounds fancy, but it's freezing in the winter. The building management keeps the AC blasting all year round anyway, so I'm always wearing this thick cardigan my sister got me for Christmas a couple of years ago. She lives up in Seattle now, so she's all about layers. I keep telling her she needs to come visit, but she hates flying. She had this terrible delay once in O'Hare where she was stuck for like twelve hours... but yeah, no, I don't feel like I'm back in time or anything, just really, really jumpy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at other times, not just when you're startled by loud noises?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. Most of the time I'm pretty relaxed at the desk, just doing my thing. I actually started doing the New York Times crossword to pass the time when it's slow. My buddy Mike got me into it. He works at this coffee shop around the corner—it's one of those independent places that charges like seven bucks for a latte, you know? They use oat milk by default now, which I just don't get. I tried it once and it honestly just tasted like leftover cereal water. I really don't know how people drink that every single morning, especially for that price. But anyway, Mike usually saves me the leftover papers from the café so I can do the puzzles. So yeah, usually I'm just sitting there working on a crossword, totally fine. It's really just when those sudden noises happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really worry about much outside of work. I go home, I watch a little TV, maybe play some video games. I actually just picked up this old console at a garage sale last weekend. It was one of those community yard sales where everyone in the neighborhood puts their stuff out on the same day. I love those. You can find some crazy stuff. This guy had a whole box of old vinyl records, mostly 80s pop, which isn't really my thing. I'm more into classic rock. My dad used to play Zeppelin all the time when he was working on his car in the garage. He had this old Mustang he was always fixing up. Never quite got it running right, though. It was always leaking oil or something. But yeah, outside of work, I'm pretty chill. I don't really sit around worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when anticipating or thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, thinking about going in... I guess a little? Mostly I just try to change how I get to my desk. Like, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can, because walking past that specific spot in the main lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it as fast as possible. So the back hallway is just easier. It routes you right past the loading dock, actually, which is where Gary, our maintenance guy, usually takes his breaks. Gary's this older guy, always wearing these heavy-duty suspenders, which you really don't see a lot of guys wearing anymore. He told me he has to order them special from some hardware catalog because the regular store-bought ones don't hold up when he's carrying his tool belt. He's a funny guy, always has a story about some pipe that burst back in the 90s. Oh, and that back hallway has this old vending machine, too—the kind with the metal coils that always get stuck? I actually lost a dollar fifty in there trying to get some Cool Ranch Doritos last Tuesday. It just totally ate my money and left the bag dangling there. I tried shaking it, but that thing weighs a ton. But anyway, yeah, I just take that route now so I don't have to go through the lobby.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think I've had anything like that. Just the startle thing at work, really. But speaking of things happening fast, my sister's dog did that the other day. She has this golden retriever mix, Buster, and he is usually the laziest dog you'll ever meet. I mean, he literally sleeps on his back with his paws in the air like a dead bug. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Which is funny because she actually buys him this really expensive organic dog food that's supposed to be made with real salmon and sweet potatoes. It smells awful, honestly. I don't know how he eats it. I told her she should just give him regular kibble, but she's really into the whole holistic pet care thing now. She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about different things in your life, not just related to work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I think we just talked about that, actually. Like I said, outside of work, I'm pretty relaxed. I don't really worry about much. Oh, except I am a little worried about my car right now. It's been making this weird rattling noise when I get up over like, 50 miles an hour. It sounds like it's coming from the front passenger side, maybe near the tire? I don't know. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment—Mike's Auto, I think it's called. Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about several different things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific at work that you worry about or fear happening?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can lately. The maintenance guy, Gary, keeps all his supplies back there. \n\nGary's a good guy, though. He actually brought in donuts on Friday. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. No, I don't think so. If I hear a loud bang or something when I'm out and about, it's just a noise, it doesn't make me feel fearful or whatever. I actually took some classes at the community college before that, thinking I'd do IT, but the benefits at this desk job are pretty decent, you know? Anyway, it used to be mostly quiet, just some accounting firms, but they recently leased the second floor to this massive tech company. And man, the amount of food delivery guys coming in and out now is insane. They never read the signs about where to leave the bags, so I'm always having to point them to the right table. \n\nBut, um, sorry, what really pushed me to make the appointment is that... well, every time one of those heavy lobby doors slams, or if someone rolls a suitcase fast across the marble floor—we get a lot of visiting consultants with those hard-shell bags now—I just jump. I jump out of my skin like it’s happening again. And it takes me a good minute just to get my heart back down to normal. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: When those loud noises startle you, do you ever feel like you're back in a different time or place?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm somewhere else or anything. I just... I get this massive jolt, you know? Like my body thinks something terrible is happening right that second. But I know I'm still at the desk. The desk is actually made of this really nice polished granite, which sounds fancy, but it's freezing in the winter. The building management keeps the AC blasting all year round anyway, so I'm always wearing this thick cardigan my sister got me for Christmas a couple of years ago. She lives up in Seattle now, so she's all about layers. I keep telling her she needs to come visit, but she hates flying. She had this terrible delay once in O'Hare where she was stuck for like twelve hours... but yeah, no, I don't feel like I'm back in time or anything, just really, really jumpy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at other times, not just when you're startled by loud noises?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. Most of the time I'm pretty relaxed at the desk, just doing my thing. I actually started doing the New York Times crossword to pass the time when it's slow. My buddy Mike got me into it. He works at this coffee shop around the corner—it's one of those independent places that charges like seven bucks for a latte, you know? They use oat milk by default now, which I just don't get. I tried it once and it honestly just tasted like leftover cereal water. I really don't know how people drink that every single morning, especially for that price. But anyway, Mike usually saves me the leftover papers from the café so I can do the puzzles. So yeah, usually I'm just sitting there working on a crossword, totally fine. It's really just when those sudden noises happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things in your daily life when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't really worry about much outside of work. I go home, I watch a little TV, maybe play some video games. I actually just picked up this old console at a garage sale last weekend. It was one of those community yard sales where everyone in the neighborhood puts their stuff out on the same day. I love those. You can find some crazy stuff. This guy had a whole box of old vinyl records, mostly 80s pop, which isn't really my thing. I'm more into classic rock. My dad used to play Zeppelin all the time when he was working on his car in the garage. He had this old Mustang he was always fixing up. Never quite got it running right, though. It was always leaking oil or something. But yeah, outside of work, I'm pretty chill. I don't really sit around worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when anticipating or thinking about going to work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, thinking about going in... I guess a little? Mostly I just try to change how I get to my desk. Like, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can, because walking past that specific spot in the main lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it as fast as possible. So the back hallway is just easier. It routes you right past the loading dock, actually, which is where Gary, our maintenance guy, usually takes his breaks. Gary's this older guy, always wearing these heavy-duty suspenders, which you really don't see a lot of guys wearing anymore. He told me he has to order them special from some hardware catalog because the regular store-bought ones don't hold up when he's carrying his tool belt. He's a funny guy, always has a story about some pipe that burst back in the 90s. Oh, and that back hallway has this old vending machine, too—the kind with the metal coils that always get stuck? I actually lost a dollar fifty in there trying to get some Cool Ranch Doritos last Tuesday. It just totally ate my money and left the bag dangling there. I tried shaking it, but that thing weighs a ton. But anyway, yeah, I just take that route now so I don't have to go through the lobby.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think I've had anything like that. Just the startle thing at work, really. But speaking of things happening fast, my sister's dog did that the other day. She has this golden retriever mix, Buster, and he is usually the laziest dog you'll ever meet. I mean, he literally sleeps on his back with his paws in the air like a dead bug. But she was over at my place, and I dropped a piece of cheese—just a tiny piece of cheddar—and I swear that dog teleported. It was gone before it even hit the floor. Which is funny because she actually buys him this really expensive organic dog food that's supposed to be made with real salmon and sweet potatoes. It smells awful, honestly. I don't know how he eats it. I told her she should just give him regular kibble, but she's really into the whole holistic pet care thing now. She even bought him this special orthopedic bed because she read that big dogs get joint problems, but he prefers sleeping on the hard tile in the kitchen anyway. So, yeah, no sudden panic attacks or anything like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about different things in your life, not just related to work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I think we just talked about that, actually. Like I said, outside of work, I'm pretty relaxed. I don't really worry about much. Oh, except I am a little worried about my car right now. It's been making this weird rattling noise when I get up over like, 50 miles an hour. It sounds like it's coming from the front passenger side, maybe near the tire? I don't know. I took it to this mechanic down the street from my apartment—Mike's Auto, I think it's called. Mike is this big guy, always has grease on his hands, no matter what time of day you go in there. He told me he thinks it might be the wheel bearing, but he wanted to charge me like four hundred dollars just to look at it, which seems crazy. I mean, I know cars are expensive to fix, but four hundred just for an inspection? My buddy Dave said he could probably look at it for me this weekend. Dave's not a real mechanic, but he watches a lot of YouTube tutorials, so he usually figures things out eventually. He fixed my sink last month after it was dripping for like two weeks. Took him three trips to Home Depot, but he got it done. But yeah, as far as just sitting around worrying about my life... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about several different things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Yeah, I'm pretty sure we just covered that. I really don't worry about a bunch of different things. I'm usually a pretty laid-back guy, you know? Like, I don't let stuff get to me. Well, except for when people don't use their turn signals. That really gets under my skin. I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, like money or relationships?\n\nPatient: Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. \n\nShe wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific at work that you worry about or fear happening?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can lately. Walking past that specific spot in the lobby where it happened... my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it. Which is a huge pain because the back hallway smells exactly like old mop water. You know that smell? Like someone left a wet sponge in a bucket for a month. The maintenance guy, Gary, keeps all his supplies back there. \n\nGary's a good guy, though. He actually brought in donuts on Friday. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. I had to use like three squirts of hand sanitizer just to answer the phones at the front desk without getting my keyboard all gross. The keyboard is already kind of gross anyway because the temp who covers my lunch breaks eats Cheetos while she types. The flaming hot ones, too. I keep finding red dust on the spacebar and it drives me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I really try to avoid that side of the lobby now.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about things like your health, finances, or relationships when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Okay, seriously, I feel like I'm on a hidden camera show or something. Are you just reading the same question off a different list? Because no, I really don't worry about my health or my bank account or relationships when I'm at home. I'm totally fine. \n\nI mean, speaking of health, I did switch to 2% milk recently instead of whole milk. My dentist actually told me to drink more milk. Which is weird, right? You'd think a regular doctor would tell you that, not a dentist. Dr. Henderson, he's a trip. He has these little TVs mounted on the ceiling above the dentist chair so you can watch Netflix while he scrapes your teeth. Last time I was there, he had on that baking show, the British one with the big white tent in the middle of a field. It's honestly so relaxing. Though it always makes me crave pastries, which kind of defeats the entire purpose of being at the dentist. Like, I left his office last Tuesday and immediately went to the bakery next door and bought a bear claw. It was totally stale, too, which was a huge letdown. You'd think a place that only sells baked goods would make sure they're fresh, but whatever. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no, I don't have any worries about that kind of stuff. When I'm not at work, I'm just chilling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble controlling your worry about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you okay? Like, genuinely, are we just doing laps around the same track here? I swear I've answered this exact thing like four times. No, I don't have trouble controlling my worry about everyday stuff, because I'm not worrying about everyday stuff. \n\nIt's actually kind of funny, my buddy Dave asks me the same questions over and over sometimes. But that's because Dave has the memory of a goldfish. We were supposed to go see that new sci-fi movie on Saturday—the one with the aliens that communicate through, like, radio static or whatever? But he completely forgot and double-booked himself for his niece's piano recital. Which, honestly, sounds like a form of torture. Sitting in a stuffy auditorium listening to a bunch of eight-year-olds butcher 'Chopsticks' for two hours? I'd rather watch paint dry. Dave said they didn't even have snacks afterward, just warm tap water in those tiny paper cones. You know the ones? The cones that you literally can't set down anywhere without spilling water all over yourself. Who even invented those? It's a terrible design. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, to answer your question for the fifth time, no. I'm not sitting around worrying about normal stuff. I'm totally fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks outside of the context of the loud noises at work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just the stuff at work. Outside of that, I'm totally fine. Like, even if there are loud noises elsewhere, it doesn't do that to me. \n\nMy neighbor across the hall, Greg, he actually just bought this massive home theater system and he was blasting some war movie on Sunday night. The bass was literally rattling my coffee table. It was super annoying, but I didn't freak out or anything. Speaking of that coffee table, though, I actually just got it at this flea market downtown. The guy selling it swore it was real mahogany, but honestly, it scratches so easily I think it's just pine with a dark stain. I had to strap it to the roof of my Civic to get it home, which was a complete nightmare because I only had those cheap bungee cords from the gas station. You know the ones where the metal hooks just bend straight if you pull them too tight? I was driving like ten miles an hour the whole way back, just waiting for it to fly off and smash into the car behind me. The guy behind me kept honking, too, which was incredibly rude, but whatever. I made it back in one piece. \n\nBut um... yeah, no, no panic attacks anywhere else. Just the work thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or fearful when you're not at work and something reminds you of the loud noises that startle you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If I hear a loud bang or something when I'm out and about, it's just a noise, it doesn't make me feel fearful or whatever. Like, the street sweeper comes down my road every Tuesday morning and it makes this horrible screeching, banging sound. It's incredibly loud. But it just annoys me, it doesn't freak me out. \n\nThe guy who drives it is always smoking this huge cigar, too, which I don't even think is allowed in city vehicles? It smells awful. It actually reminds me of my Uncle Frank. He used to smoke those cheap cigars at family barbecues and the smell would just cling to your clothes for days. One time he was telling this wild story and waving his hands around, and he accidentally burned a hole right through my little cousin's inflatable pool with the cherry of his cigar. It was hilarious, honestly, watching all the water just flood out into the yard and completely ruin my aunt's petunias. She was so mad she didn't speak to him for like a month. She takes her gardening super seriously. She even won a ribbon at the county fair for her prize-winning squash, which I didn't even know was a real thing outside of cartoons. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, it's really just the loud noises at work. Nothing else bothers me like that. 

I mean, I sit at the front desk all day, so I see all kinds of crazy situations that would probably stress other people out, but I just brush them off. Like on Tuesday, this courier came in with a hand truck stacked way too high with heavy boxes, and he completely jammed the front revolving door. Just wedged it solid. People were backed up into the lobby, and our maintenance guy, Jerry, had to come up with his tools to unstick it. Jerry is a total character, man. He's always complaining about his bad knees and how the building management won't buy him the right kind of floor wax. He spent like twenty minutes telling me about the chemical composition of industrial wax while he was prying this door open. Apparently, if it doesn't have enough carnauba in it, it scuffs if you just look at it wrong. I don't even know what a carnauba is, honestly. It sounds like some kind of tropical bird or maybe a type of fruit? I should probably Google that later. 

But uh... yeah, what were we saying? Oh, right, other situations. No, I don't get distressed about anything else. It's literally just the noise thing at work. Everything else is completely fine.

I was driving behind this guy in a silver Honda Civic yesterday, and he literally changed lanes like four times without signaling once. Just swerving all over the place. And he had this bumper sticker that said \"My other ride is the Millennium Falcon,\" which is kind of funny, I guess, but it doesn't give you an excuse to drive like a jerk. I actually saw the new Star Wars movie last weekend with my cousin. We get popcorn, but he always insists on getting the jalapeño slices on the side and pouring the juice over the popcorn. It makes it all soggy, but he swears by it. He says he learned it from a guy he used to work with at a movie theater in high school. But anyway, no, I don't really sit around worrying about things in my day-to-day life. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, like money or relationships?\n\nPatient: Wait, seriously, doc? Are you testing my memory or something? I feel like you've asked me this like three times now in different ways. No, I don't worry about money or relationships or everyday stuff. I mean, dating is whatever. I actually went on a date a couple weeks ago with this woman I met on one of those apps—Bumble, I think, the one where they have to message you first. \n\nShe wanted to go to this strict vegan place downtown. I'm not vegan, but I figured I'd keep an open mind, right? They had this stuff called 'facon,' which is like fake bacon made out of soy or tempeh or whatever. It honestly tasted exactly like a burnt piece of cardboard. Like, it just turned to dust in my mouth. And the waiter kept coming over to ask how the 'plant-based journey' was going. He was wearing this ridiculous straw fedora indoors, which always bothers me. My grandpa used to say it was incredibly rude to wear a hat at the dinner table. He was in the Navy, so he was super strict about rules like that. He actually served on a submarine for a few years, which sounds absolutely terrifying. Just being trapped in a metal tube underwater for months? No thank you. \n\nBut anyway... yeah, no, my finances and love life are fine. No worrying there.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific at work that you worry about or fear happening?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, I've been taking the back hallway whenever I can lately. Walking past that specific spot in the lobby where it happened... my chest just gets all tight and I just want to get away from it. Which is a huge pain because the back hallway smells exactly like old mop water. You know that smell? Like someone left a wet sponge in a bucket for a month. The maintenance guy, Gary, keeps all his supplies back there. \n\nGary's a good guy, though. He actually brought in donuts on Friday. Maple glazed, which are usually my favorite, but he got them from that place on 4th Street, and they always put way too much glaze on them. Your fingers get completely sticky. I had to use like three squirts of hand sanitizer just to answer the phones at the front desk without getting my keyboard all gross. The keyboard is already kind of gross anyway because the temp who covers my lunch breaks eats Cheetos while she types. The flaming hot ones, too. I keep finding red dust on the spacebar and it drives me crazy. \n\nBut yeah, I just... Are you just reading the same question off a different list? Because no, I really don't worry about my health or my bank account or relationships when I'm at home. I'm totally fine. \n\nI mean, speaking of health, I did switch to 2% milk recently instead of whole milk. My dentist actually told me to drink more milk. Which is weird, right? You'd think a regular doctor would tell you that, not a dentist. Dr. Henderson, he's a trip. He has these little TVs mounted on the ceiling above the dentist chair so you can watch Netflix while he scrapes your teeth. Last time I was there, he had on that baking show, the British one with the big white tent in the middle of a field. It's honestly so relaxing. Though it always makes me crave pastries, which kind of defeats the entire purpose of being at the dentist. Like, I left his office last Tuesday and immediately went to the bakery next door and bought a bear claw. It was totally stale, too, which was a huge letdown. You'd think a place that only sells baked goods would make sure they're fresh, but whatever. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no, I don't have any worries about that kind of stuff. When I'm not at work, I'm just chilling.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble controlling your worry about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you okay? Like, genuinely, are we just doing laps around the same track here? I swear I've answered this exact thing like four times. No, I don't have trouble controlling my worry about everyday stuff, because I'm not worrying about everyday stuff. \n\nIt's actually kind of funny, my buddy Dave asks me the same questions over and over sometimes. But that's because Dave has the memory of a goldfish. We were supposed to go see that new sci-fi movie on Saturday—the one with the aliens that communicate through, like, radio static or whatever? But he completely forgot and double-booked himself for his niece's piano recital. Which, honestly, sounds like a form of torture. Sitting in a stuffy auditorium listening to a bunch of eight-year-olds butcher 'Chopsticks' for two hours? I'd rather watch paint dry. Dave said they didn't even have snacks afterward, just warm tap water in those tiny paper cones. You know the ones? The cones that you literally can't set down anywhere without spilling water all over yourself. Who even invented those? It's a terrible design. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, to answer your question for the fifth time, no. I'm not sitting around worrying about normal stuff. I'm totally fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks outside of the context of the loud noises at work?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just the stuff at work. Outside of that, I'm totally fine. Like, even if there are loud noises elsewhere, it doesn't do that to me. \n\nMy neighbor across the hall, Greg, he actually just bought this massive home theater system and he was blasting some war movie on Sunday night. The bass was literally rattling my coffee table. It was super annoying, but I didn't freak out or anything. Speaking of that coffee table, though, I actually just got it at this flea market downtown. The guy selling it swore it was real mahogany, but honestly, it scratches so easily I think it's just pine with a dark stain. I had to strap it to the roof of my Civic to get it home, which was a complete nightmare because I only had those cheap bungee cords from the gas station. You know the ones where the metal hooks just bend straight if you pull them too tight? I was driving like ten miles an hour the whole way back, just waiting for it to fly off and smash into the car behind me. The guy behind me kept honking, too, which was incredibly rude, but whatever. I made it back in one piece. \n\nBut um... yeah, no, no panic attacks anywhere else. Just the work thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or fearful when you're not at work and something reminds you of the loud noises that startle you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. If I hear a loud bang or something when I'm out and about, it's just a noise, it doesn't make me feel fearful or whatever. Like, the street sweeper comes down my road every Tuesday morning and it makes this horrible screeching, banging sound. It's incredibly loud. But it just annoys me, it doesn't freak me out. \n\nThe guy who drives it is always smoking this huge cigar, too, which I don't even think is allowed in city vehicles? It smells awful. It actually reminds me of my Uncle Frank. He used to smoke those cheap cigars at family barbecues and the smell would just cling to your clothes for days. One time he was telling this wild story and waving his hands around, and he accidentally burned a hole right through my little cousin's inflatable pool with the cherry of his cigar. It was hilarious, honestly, watching all the water just flood out into the yard and completely ruin my aunt's petunias. She was so mad she didn't speak to him for like a month. She takes her gardening super seriously. She even won a ribbon at the county fair for her prize-winning squash, which I didn't even know was a real thing outside of cartoons. \n\nBut um... wait, what was the question? I'm fine.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety or fear only about the loud noises at work, or are there other situations that cause you similar distress?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the loud noises at work. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. I'm pretty good with people. You kind of have to be when you're working the front desk, you know? Meeting new people is just part of the gig. I talk to delivery guys, clients, random people asking for directions... it doesn't bother me at all. 

Actually, speaking of people asking for directions, this older couple came in last week looking for the DMV. We are nowhere near the DMV. I have no idea how they ended up in our lobby. The husband had this huge paper map—like an actual fold-out map, not a phone—and he was tracing the roads with this giant magnifying glass. I haven't seen a real paper map in years. It reminded me of when my dad used to take us on road trips to Florida when I was a kid. He refused to ask for directions, ever. We ended up lost in the Everglades once because he swore he knew a shortcut. My mom was absolutely terrified we were going to get eaten by alligators. We had this old station wagon with the wood paneling on the side, and the AC was broken, so we had all the windows down, which probably wasn't the smartest move if you're worried about alligators. My sister kept crying that she had to go to the bathroom, but there was nowhere to stop except for these creepy swamp shacks. We finally found a gas station that sold boiled peanuts, which are surprisingly good if you get the spicy ones. 

But um... yeah, social situations. No anxiety there. I'm totally fine around people.
